That also means for the future: The shorter you formulate a main question, the more time you have to justify this main question as well. ParlaMint-AT_2014-02-25-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00014_d8e4417	Good morning, Madam President. Federal Chancellor! We've been making debt for about 50 years, and we've been making sure new debts every year. My question to you: How do you think you will do this, given the not fully calculated hypo-debakel? The request submitted in writing, 11/M, has the following wording: [[Quoted print version removed]]
ParlaMint-AT_2014-02-25-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00014_d8e4449	Let us be generous and let us now apply this question. But also for you, Mr Klubobmann, the rule applies: The main question has to be rendered true to words. – Mr Bundeskanzler, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2014-02-25-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00014_d8e4479	I would like to reply briefly in two parts. First: Yes, we have to reduce debt, which is why we have also decided on a debt brake in this House – unfortunately only by a majority, but after all. Secondly, The level of debt alone does not yet state what it is all about the economic power of a country. The best example is our German Neighbor. No one in the world doubts that Germany is the strongest or one of the strongest economic nations, especially in Europe, and yet you know that debt in Germany is percent higher than in Austria. So the debt level alone is an important size, but it is truly not the only size to make a statement about the economic power of a country. It should therefore be sought that the structural deficit – that is, what is going on permanently – will come to zero in a country. We remain at our 2016 target, and at the hour we cannot say, for example, in an institution solution, whether there is an impact on the structural deficit, and if so in what form. In fact, an institution solution – to name just one example of the many possibilities – means an increase in debt, but not a direct structural deficit. So the impact can only be assessed if the models are in implementation. Where you are right, it is important to achieve this goal of structural zero deficit in 2016 because it provides scope for action for stability. And stability is not a self-interest, but rather a lower interest rate and a much more meaningful spending of money in the country.

[[10:15:42]] 1. Point Report of the Committee on Agriculture and Forestry on the Green Report 2013 from the Federal Government (III-26/41 d.a.)
ParlaMint-AT_2014-02-25-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00014_d8e6154	We're now on the 1st. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Jannach is the first to speak. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2014-02-25-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00014_d8e6182	Madam President. Federal Minister! The agenda today is rather poor. We have a single law to adopt under item 2, namely the Environmental Impact Assessment Act. Under item 1 of the agenda, we are discussing the Green Report on the situation in agriculture, unfortunately from 2012. The figures are no longer the latest. However, the Green Report, which is the issue I have here, in fact - I want to go ahead with this - provides a good basis for analyzing the situation in agriculture. It is informative and above all very extensive. This is particularly important this year, because it is a crucial year for Austrian agriculture. It is about the new funding scene, about the CAP reform, i.e. the common agricultural policy in Europe. It is one of the few areas that are being developed in Europe in common, and this is why it is particularly important to discuss this new funding for the next six or seven years in particular. Briefly from the Green Report: On page 9 the whole development of agriculture has already been described, under the beautiful title “agricultural structure” – I gather: The results of the agricultural structural survey – which is the collection of all agricultural data – show that there are about 173,000 farmers, but that, compared with the full survey in 1999, that is, ten years ago, the number of farmers decreased by around 20 percent. Since Austria's accession to the EU, 27.5% of farmers have quit their operations. This shows the whole of the drama of structural change in agriculture, and it is therefore particularly important that we try to put in place appropriate measures that really counter farmers' deaths, as they are commonly termed. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] In fact, we have a development that runs out of growth or shifts. This can be disputed or not, at least it does. The situation is particularly dramatic in the milk sector. We have lost more than 40 percent of active dairy farmers in the last ten years. – I mention this because the non-agricultural population needs to understand what it is about in agriculture. Agriculture not only means getting support and collecting as much funding as possible, but also makes an essential contribution to social life in agriculture. Austria. It should be borne in mind that the abandonment or disappearance of agricultural holdings leads to non-management, in particular from remote regions, especially in the mountain area. There is a loss of safe food production in these areas with all social developments, and this, in the final analysis, also leads to a loss of secure food production. Everything shifts to favors. We are particularly highlighting this area of freedom. We want to maintain agriculture in the mountain region, especially small-scale agriculture, and therefore we need to promote it intensively with the new programme. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] This is a crucial point: Today the corresponding negotiations are under way, and they will be concluded this year. And we have some serious criticisms on the draft which the Minister has presented to us. In principle, substantial resources are deleted. There are two pillars for agriculture: on the one hand, direct payments for the land and on the other hand, large-scale public-sector measures on rural development. In the field of direct payments, we have a minus of 4 percent, which is almost €200 million, and we also have a minus in the second pillar, where these environmental measures are concerned. As a particularly dramatic one, the Obmann of Bio Austria – Bio is a strong area in Austrian agriculture – is to fear cuts of more than 30 percent. The Minister has rejected this in the Committee. He said these figures were not right. We hope that the Minister is right, because if we had 30 percent loss of funding in the organic sector, that would have a really dramatic impact on this agricultural sector. But the really unjust – and on the other hand we have kept ourselves in committee – is the continuation of the so-called historical model. This must be explained to the non-agricultural population: Currently, farmers receive support regardless of the way in which they manage because of past reference years, years in which this support has been measured. That means in concrete terms: In the federal state of Tyrol, for example, we have a funding amount of 130 to 150 € per hectare; in this area we have subsidies of 400 € per hectare and beyond. In our view, this has been very, very unfair for ten and more years, because a reference period is being used. Now, in the presentation of the Minister and the Federal Government, it is true that this model is being changed, but not to the extent that we imagine it. It is only in five years that the new regional model will produce a level playing field between all farmers. In our view, this takes too long. We demand that the system be changed earlier and that justice in the field of operating premiums and payment entitlements be faster. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] What we welcome, Mr Minister – and I can also praise it – is that we intend to increase the promotion of young buyers, and that is also intended to increase investment support. In particular, investment promotion is one of the essential measures in the agricultural sector, which not only helps the agricultural sector but also all the others. We could start with: Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I should like to thank the rapporteur for his excellent report. It is not necessary – I also said this in committee – to promote hobby or craft courses through the LFI. We could actually save there and use the funding for those areas that really help active farmers. Moreover, it seems essential to us that we finally separate the funding for farmers and the funding for public authorities. There is always the impression that farmers get all the money, but in reality it is that many public bodies – the agricultural market Austria, the agriculture ministry itself, the agricultural chambers – take advantage of many of these farmers' funds, unfortunately God. In the public presentation, it always looks like farmers are the promoters. In fact, they receive a much lower share of compensation than the total amount in the field of public support. We do not dispute that these institutions can also be promoted, but we believe that it should be separated, that the support for agriculture, compensatory payments and public bodies should be separated. We should try to change that together. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] I do not want to ignore – and this has nothing to do with the CAP reform now, but I would also like to point it out again and again – the burden of the 2012 savings package. Mr. Federal Minister, you are not responsible for this, but I have to mention it again and again. In the 2012 savings package, farmers were actively taken away income – you know – that agricultural diesel promotion, for example, € 50 million a year. This is not so much about promoting, but about a level playing field in Europe. There are many countries in the European Union. This agricultural diesel promotion or the refund on mineral oil, as we call it in the field, and we therefore have a disparity between the Austrian farmers. That is why we insist that this support, which is directly income-effective, be reintroduced. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] A great burden – and we know that too – is the social security contributions, which are massive in terms of farmers' incomes. There was a massive increase with the 2012 savings package. This has nothing to do with the EU's agricultural reform, but it is a home-made matter of the Federal Government. We ask you to take real measures to reduce the burden on farmers through the social security contribution. This is about €80 million, which is – for farmers of older years – more than 1 billion schilling, which is due to additional loads. Furthermore: 10 million increase, this was the fifth prescribing in the previous year, in contributions for agricultural and forestry farms. – This is also a burden, which is not EU-related, but purely homemade. I also mention the re-establishment of the unit values. The re-establishment of unit values will also lead to an increase. For the non-agricultural population, you have to translate this: The uniform values are the basis for all the taxes to be borne by agriculture. If there is an increase of 10 per cent, as planned on average, all taxes and contributions are also increased for farmers. This must be seen again and again in the context of the Green Report, according to which the number of farmers is more than going down. I would also like to address an area that concerns farmers in a massive way and also ask the Minister to become active, namely in the field of agricultural management. It cannot be said that more and more people are involved in the management and handling of farmers' concerns than there are active farmers. We have a decline of almost 30 percent over the last nearly 20 years in the number of farmers, but we have an explosion-like development in the field of agriculture management. We therefore ask you to really save in the administration and try to bring as much money as possible to the active farmers. Only a short number: We have noted 125 000 farms in Austria, invekos farms, and there are 173 000 in total. We have 70 000 test reports prepared by the agricultural market Austria, i.e. the control authority, and 19 000 farms have been controlled. It always says that only 5 percent have to be controlled – 19 000 businesses are 15 percent, which were controlled annually or at least in 2012! We really urge you to put in place measures in the field of support, which can also be controlled – at present, we have measures that are hardly controllable, that is simply to say – and to try to remove controls where control costs more than the promotion at all. So we demand a lower limit of funding, where it is not controlled, because it makes no sense if the control of a funding of €200 costs at an amount of € 1,000. In this context, we really ask you to take appropriate measures in the area of administration. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] In principle, I would also like to mention that work in the Committee on Agriculture has at present become a better one; we have been used to it in the last five years. I believe that this will be confirmed by all representatives of the opposition parties in the Committee on Agriculture. Mr Minister, you have taken time for us, that was very unusual, you have provided documents that we have not received for five years, and you are trying to respond to our proposals. Even if our proposals have been postponed in the Committee, at least the willingness has been signaled to involve us. I would like to address the following request, which is jointly tabled by the government parties and parts of the opposition, which also concerns rural development and the corresponding measures. We support this request, but I hope that it will not only remain in these general terms, but that these words will also follow. This concerns in particular the expansion of investment promotion and the strengthening of mountain farms in Zones 3 and 4. We fully agree that we should strengthen these areas. As has been said, Mr Minister, the request of the liberals is to save in the administration, to bring the money to those who actively shape Austrian society in the agricultural sector, to try to save in the administration, and to try to formulate measures in such a way that they are verifiable, verifiable and also understandable to a general public. The image of agriculture has suffered massively in recent years because of these support debates, and I do not think that our farmers deserve to this extent as it has been done. Thank you.

A few numbers: As a tourism and leisure factor, the equestrian economy in Austria generates a production of up to €2.1 billion, creating up to 23 000 jobs. I believe that in the time of high unemployment this is a very impressive number. Now the new regulation has resulted in farmers who are employed in their stable horses having to pay 20 percent VAT since the beginning of the year. This is for I – and not only for me – really, very difficult to understand. [[Congratulations on the Stronach team.]] On the one hand, it is an unequal treatment, because the attitude of horses is similar to that of cows. They eat the same hay, they have the same place, and on the other hand they punish the youth, private riding and animal husbandry because this tax must ultimately be passed on to the horse-keepers. That's what I'm asking myself: Why? It is much more in the public interest to promote sports, animal-friendly horse-keeping and reasonable youth activity in Austria. Unfortunately, this regulation is the very opposite. [[Congratulations on the Stronach team.]] This, of course, does not only cause farmers to lose their jobs, but also many others around the horse economic factor, and the whole is also underlined by the fact that those who are going to sell the new turnover tax are even failing to deduct the tax. All in all, it is obvious that this package of measures is not only against farmers, but also against riders. Mr Minister, we have already been allowed to talk about this subject in advance. I know you're just like me and many, many others. We put very large pieces on you and hope you fix the mistakes of your predecessors and do something good in the sense of the farmers and the horse sports enthusiasts. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-02-25-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00014_d8e10769	Mr Magnificent came to speak. It's a kilnauer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2014-02-25-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00014_d8e10791	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! Appreciate Seers and Advisors! I would like to make it clear here once again why the last amendment to the Environmental Impact Assessment Act was necessary. As we know, at the beginning of the year, the Federal Administrative Court replaced the Environmental Senate as a judicial agency in EIA proceedings. And in the course of the corresponding revision of the EIA legislation, it has been established for already ongoing procedures that: Complaints against administrative decisions in the case of federal road construction projects do not have a suspensive effect. The concept of delaying effect is somewhat formalistic. What does that mean? – It means that the rights may not be exercised from a decision, because the ordinary appeal appeal has a so-called suspensive effect. For example, a building must not be built if, on the other hand, appeals have been made; the construction site has to wait for the decision of the appeal authority. However, a house building cannot be compared with procedures that are handled in EIA procedures, because construction processes are relatively short and the construction authorities in our communities also ensure that they are handled quickly. Infrastructure projects, especially those in the size of EIA authorisation procedures, are mostly planned and reviewed for a long time by experts with appropriate time and resources. These procedures are also used by parties to protect their rights, and water law, the environment, nature conservation are examined and taken into account. Where an appeal against a decision taken in such proceedings is suspensive, a further extension of the procedure shall be expected until a final decision is taken. The present proposal to the Environmental Impact Assessment Act now includes legal remedies against high performance decisions – and now comes into proceedings before the 31st century. ==References====External links== The legal situation in force on December 2013 would not have been allowed to give rise to a suspensive effect. This too concerns, as it were, only existing procedures and corresponds to the up to 31. This Regulation shall enter into force on the day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. It cannot therefore be said that the legal order would be dismantled. On the contrary, there is a need to achieve equality with the Federal Roads Procedure, because it is not possible to see why high-performance lines should be treated differently in this area. I cannot understand the allegation of the restriction of civil rights, too, since the decision of the first instance will be reviewed without delay of a complaint. It is not intended to delay proceedings by making a complaint. In any case, the builder builds on his own risk, because he has to adapt his construction project according to the decision of the Federal Administrative Court or, as it used to be, the Administrative Court if it amends or withdraws the first instance notice. This also means, of course, that the builder will not start construction if he is not sufficiently convinced that he will keep it. Due to my personal experience, I can fully support these changes. This change is an acceleration of construction projects, unnecessary costs are saved, acceleration is to be expected and planning and legal certainty has been restored for the procedures already under way. – Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-02-25-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00014_d8e11739	Ladies and gentlemen! But I think it is good if we get into the debate seriously, because what we are discussing here is a real constitutional, constitutional deficit. I remember the Fiscal Pact. The Fiscal Pact was a contract of international law, which has largely bound Austria. This debate has occurred in connection with the Fiscal Pact. This was simply implemented here in Parliament and then challenged later. At that time, the Constitutional Court Finally, the Fiscal Pact is unconstitutional, but Austria would no longer have come out of this Treaty under international law. The Constitutional Court's decision that the Fiscal Pact is not unconstitutional is to be accepted, but the question will arise again and again. That is, there is only one way out of this legal protection dilemma, and that is this preliminary examination. If we were to implement a contract under international law, we would either decide by a two-thirds majority – and then it would be ratified – or – if a majority came to the conclusion that it would simply be implemented by law – it would simply be implemented and ratified, and then we would be in the case of legal protection, because then there is no way of getting out of international law. Such a contract would be unconstitutional within the country. I therefore believe that we can get seriously into this debate. Whether the 20 or more Members can ask who should have the right to apply can all be discussed, but this problem must be addressed seriously. There is no alternative to pre-examination. And once there is the opinion – this opinion goes from the President of the Federal Republic of Germany to the Greens – that this is a sensible initiative, then, I believe, it is indeed so. In this sense, we should take this seriously, take the necessary steps here and allow Parliament's preliminary examination so that we do not fall into the constitutional trap. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-02-25-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00014_d8e11921	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Appreciate viewers! This includes Croatia, Roma and Hungary in Burgenland, Slovenians in Carinthia and Styria, as well as Croatia, Roma, Slovaks, Czechs and Hungary in Vienna. In international conventions, Austria has undertaken, among other things, to enable and support members of the recognised groups of school education in the mother tongue. The Council of Europe notes deficits for Austria. The Council of Europe's Ministerial Committee has repeatedly urged Austria to ensure that the increasing demand for education in the national language groups is satisfied. This deficit has also been recognised by the Federal Capital of Vienna, which is concerned primarily with it. In a statement by the Office of the Vienna State Government in the review procedure for a new People's Group Act, the German federal government supports a systematic legal promotion of private schools for the self-employed groups in Vienna. This solution has obviously also been followed by SPÖ and Greens in the Constitutional Convention. The request made by us in front of you is a pragmatic solution approach, because it closes a supply gap and ends a disadvantage to those private schools that are not operated by churches or religious communities. He does not yet mean equality with the minority school laws in Carinthia and Burgenland; that would actually be the desired goal. Our request, however, is at least a cost-acceptance by the Federal Government for the Private Schools of the People's Groups and the abolition of gross discrimination against those members of the ethnic groups who are not covered by these two minority school laws for Carinthia and Burgenland. Our request also means the abolition of a gross discrimination against private schools of ethnic groups vis-à-vis those who have been financially supported by the federal government for decades on account of the concordat. The proposal for a law follows the recommendations of the Council of Europe and a real need, which can be illustrated by a few figures. In Styria, for example, nearly 500 students attend Slovenian lessons, only as an outdoor object or as a non-binding exercise. In the already mentioned Comensky schools in Vienna, nearly 400 students participate in bilingual classes in German and Czech and German and Slovak respectively. So far as the material, legalist background of this proposal is concerned, but what is it about? – It is about keeping in mind that, especially in times of globalisation, multilingualism is one of the most important competences with which we can equip our young generation, namely the competence that a person needs to find himself in the new world and live in a modern working world. We have a concrete proposal for action here, which can help us to equip the young generation with it, because we have the potential to make the next generation Europe fit. We have the opportunity to improve the opportunities of the next generation, to prove ourselves on the labour market and to find the jobs corresponding to their qualifications. We do not need to reinvent the wheel, we simply need to implement and expand the existing ideas. Austria is located in the heart of the European Union, surrounded by cultures and languages that have been known to us for centuries and yet very strange. Although some 90 million Europeans speak German as their native language, 440 million do not. Therefore, our motto must be: Let's get the chance! Izkoristimo priložnost! Let's take the chance! Prendimo l’occasione! Saisissons la chance! In this sense: Madame et Messieurs, merci bien pour votre attention!

Point First reading: Request by Members Georg Willi, colleagues on a federal law amending the Federal Roads Act (115/A)
ParlaMint-AT_2014-02-25-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00014_d8e12295	We're on the 5th. Agenda item. We are going into the debate. President. — The next item is the joint debate on the following motions for resolutions: - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2014-02-25-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00014_d8e12322	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! It is about the federal road toll law and about a novel that we wanted to do about it. The background is a fact in Kufstein, which the Bregenzers know well, and it is about the fact that the motorway and highway network in Austria is naturally compulsory by means of the so-called vignette. Especially in the room Kufstein there is a lot of traffic from the area of southern Bavaria to the recreation areas of Kitzbühel. To get there, it is best to drive 6 kilometres on the motorway and then on the federal roads to the district of Kitzbühel. It was similar in Bregenz. Although there has been the obligation of vignettes since 1997, this section of the motorway has not been controlled to prevent negative evasion on the low-ranking road network. So in the same legal situation, far over ten years have not been controlled, and suddenly the Federal Minister comes and says that now she has to check. The rise in the population was large, because it was feared that there would be a lot of evasion traffic, which actually occurred, up to 47 percent more traffic on the low-level road network, with this traffic actually running on the motorway. Clear: We of the Greens are in favour of paying for motorways. Only if the negative effects of these controls are so large that the damage is greater than the benefit, then you have to look at it, and this request is aimed at preventing such a thing by means of a legal regulation – and that is temporary. So if there are two conditions – first of all special local conditions as in Kufstein and second in time – the Federal Minister should be able to waive the control of vignettes by means of a regulation. That's the part that comes to the negotiation. We would not only help the population in Kufstein, but also the population at other places on the motorway network, where there are exactly the same negative effects. A word on the subject of vignette. Let's be honest, the vignette reminds a little of the stamp: You have to buy them laboriously, you have to pick them, and when they run out, you have to scratch them down. [[Call the ÖVP:Only work!]] The Germans and other holidaymakers do not like this because they usually do not buy an annual vignette, but a two-week vignette. That means going somewhere where you get this vignette at all, then you have to pick it up, and after 14 days it doesn't apply anymore, and then you have to scratch it effortlessly again. That is to say, the vignette system itself is completely obsolete, no longer modern, and secondly, it is impossible at all to understand why the vignette, no matter if you drive a lot or a little, is equally expensive for all. That's not performance dependent, everyone just pays. That's like a ticket, but if you drive 10 kilometers or 1,000 miles, you're totally squandered, you pay the same amount. That is to be resolved. This is what we are working on and, fortunately, we are working on many other good people, right up to the ASFINAG, which is already starting to think about how to do it better. – In short, it is about improving the existing vignette system in Austria.
ParlaMint-AT_2014-02-25-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00014_d8e12359	The next speaker was Mr Hell. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2014-02-25-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00014_d8e12380	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! The applicant has already pointed out that this proposal for an initiative aims at empowering BMVIT to adopt, in agreement with the Federal Ministry of Finance, a regulation which, on certain sections of the high-speed road network, suspends the controls of the time-dependent toll with the aim of avoiding unwanted transport relocations on low-level road networks. Ladies and gentlemen! The Bundesstraßen-Mautgesetz currently prescribes beautifications on the entire Austrian highway and motorway network. This means that, with the exception of the special toll lines, the entire high-level road network is subject to a uniform vignette system. It does not provide for any special arrangements for certain groups or individual border crossings. A derogation, motivated by Kufstein, but generally formulated, would result in a variety of follow-up claims in comparable cases in the case of an exception in the Kufstein sector. For example, the Urban Federation has already pointed out that it demands exceptions in the urban area. However, the Urban Federation has not argued that, of course, if such a derogation were to take place for certain cities, the Greater Region of Vienna would also have to be considered here. Such a special arrangement would therefore affect nearly 300 kilometres of the road system, which is about 15 percent of the total road network. This would call into question the vignette system as a whole, and I believe that these are points that we should discuss and consider together in the Committee.

The Task Force should set up an expert body to support the Federal Government in all matters of restructuring and decommissioning of Hypo Alpe-Adria-Bank-International AG through its expertise and competence. The Task Force consists of well-known experts sent by the National Bank, the Financial Market Supervision, the Federal Ministry of Finance and the FIMBAG. After Dr. Klaus Liebscher, the president of the National Bank University Professor Dr. Ewald Novotny has now taken over the task force. As the co-ordinator of the Hypo Alpe-Adria also takes power. Georg Krakow as an independent expert at a renowned Vienna law firm participating in the consultations. The task force was entrusted with the concrete and important task of undergoing a detailed and accurate analysis of all types of hypo-alpe-Adria. The different reduction variants were developed internally by the Bank in a large-scale project. The Task Force will prepare and analyse the various models and draw up detailed recommendations. On the basis of these recommendations, the Austrian Federal Government will take its decision. Question 10: ==References== On 3 March 2002, the Federal Ministry of Finance was the competent supervisory authority for the Hypo Alpe-Adria. ==References== In April 2002, the Financial Markets Supervisory Board took up its operational operation as an independent and non-profit supervisory authority and thus took up the tasks of auditing and drawing up audit reports. It is clear that the supervisory acts before 2002 are no longer relevant to the current situation. In the following years, I would like to point out that both the FMA and the National Bank are independent authorities and are not subject to the law of the Federal Ministry of Finance. Their activities are not subject to the control of the content of the Ministry of Finance. The Ministry of Finance does not have any enforcement competence in terms of operational banking supervision, which also excludes access to the extensive supervisory data of the OeNB or the FMA. The function of the Federal Ministry of Finance in the supervisory system is limited to the supervision of the FMA in accordance with § 16 Financial Market Supervision Act. The information right there, inter alia, which is standardised, can only be exercised in the event of justified doubts as to the legality of the supervisory activity. Two such requests to the FMA have been made in connection with the Hypo Alpe-Adria. In the course of the rescue negotiations in December 2009, financial market supervision was requested to present its supervisory activities in the years 2006 to 2009, and for the second time, following an indication by financial market supervision, that the then Chairman of the Board of Management at the meeting of the Supervisory Board of Hypo Alpe-Adria of 8. On March 1, 2011, it was thought that the responsibility of owners and supervisors should also be examined in the process of preparing the past. There is likely to have been a decision-making and supervisory failure, which is why financial market supervision was requested to explain its supervisory measures adopted since 2005 aimed at improving the risk management systems of Hypo Alpe-Adria. However, the opinion of the Financial Market Supervisory Board showed that the presumption of the Chairman of the Board of Management at that time could not be confirmed. On the question 11: In addition to my previous reply to this question, I would also like to point out that the supervision of the credit conduct of the Hypo Alpe-Adria is the responsibility of independent and non-profit-making supervisory authority and not of my ministry. In addition, I would like to point out that a detailed assessment of the balance sheets and associated with them is also the assessment of assets and value adjustments of outstanding exposures, such as loans, the task of the auditors. Question 12: The work of the past is mainly done by the bank itself. It has also contributed external consultants to the Bank's institutions if necessary and after they have been made willing to do so. At the Federal Ministry of Finance, I have already mentioned this, there is neither the cost of the individual consultants nor that of the total cost. There are also no details of specific calls for tenders. Therefore, there can also be no cost-benefit monitoring from my ministry. On the question 13: Immediately after the emergency nationalization in December 2009, the project CSI Hypo was set up to rework the past in Hypo Alpe-Adria and in particular to clarify the causes of the Bank's economic failure. The implementation of this project was primarily the responsibility of the Bank's institutions, i.e. of the Executive Board and Supervisory Board. The CSI Hypo project has already shown numerous different and complex facts about intensive investigations by the public prosecutor. However, a final report is still outstanding. Question 15: The Financial Procuratorship as a lawyer of the Republic was of course involved in the contractual negotiations. On the question 16: This would have to be explained first of all as to what are ‘essential parts of the contract and the terms of the contract’, but it seems that a special attention will be paid to the question of the guarantee. Of course, the right to challenge the share purchase contract is granted to the Republic of Austria regardless of the guarantee. The Federal Ministry of Finance therefore asked the institutions of Hypo Alpe-Adria AG at an early stage for the purpose of clarifying a possible challenge to the emergency contract for the comprehensive examination of the Bank's actual state at the time of the emergency. To this end, comprehensive external evaluations have been commissioned by the Bank. In particular, they assess the economic situation of the Hypo Alpe-Adria at the time of the emergency and, subsequently, a subsequent capitalisation requirement of the Hypo Alpe-Adria which had not yet occurred at the time and which had not been known to the Republic of Austria in the event of the acquisition of shares. On the question 17: It should be noted that the provisions of the Emergency Treaty require the Republic of Austria only to communicate early on with the Bavarian Landesbank of a planned split-up of the Hypo Alpe-Adria, as would be necessary for the establishment of a Bad Bank. Only in such a case can the Bayerische Landesbank demand the repayment of the refinancing lines still existing at the bank from the Hypo Alpe-Adria. Of course, the Republic of Austria reserves the right to appeal against the Bavarian Landesbank for an appropriate time. I have already pointed out in the past that we still have time to do so this year through a renunciation of limitation. Questions 18 and 19: The effects of the possible design of a solution for the Hypo Alpe-Adria on creditors are currently being examined. As already stated in the last urgent request, there is no updated register with concrete names of the bond holders. The bonds are also traded in the secondary market and are therefore subject to a constant and steady change. Question 20: There was a strong initiative in December 2009 shortly before the emergency nationalisation of the European Central Bank to prevent the hypo-Alpe-Adria from falling down in order to prevent massive disruptions on the European financial market and a threatening domino effect. In December 2009, the Oesterreichische National Bank clearly rated the Hypo Alpe-Adria as a system-relevant and insisted on a solution. The National Bank called in particular the uncontrollable and calculable infectious effects in the Austrian banking sector, the massive impact on the economies of south-eastern Europe and the immensely high liabilities of the country of Carinthia. Questions 21 to 23: National and international experts have analysed the business, economic and legal conditions and have developed models and scenarios from them to find the best possible solution for taxation and economics. Oliver Wyman assessed the scenarios developed by these experts. In addition, however, there is a very concrete recommendation from the national experts of the Task Force. On the side of the Task Force a cascade of models was proposed. The first model should examine the feasibility of the so-called banking participation model. But it is known to you – I don't have to do this – that this model proved to be unrealisable. Therefore, the next model recommended by the experts of the Taskforce, that of setting up a so-called institution, is now examined. As details of the structure, scope and design of the institution model are currently being developed at expert level, no serious assessment can yet be made of the specific effects. Only after the expert's report is available can a decision be taken on the establishment and scope of an establishment solution as well as on the possibilities for third parties to participate. Any other options are not a taboo, but are currently working intensively on checking the feasibility of an institution solution. The current situation regarding the extent of the liability is known. At present, the European Commission has approved the Federal Government's liabilities of around €1.2 billion, and the federal state of Carinthia has a liability of €12.4 billion. Question 24: The scale of the costs that the Hypo Alpe-Adria has caused for the Republic of Austria and thus for the Austrian taxpayer and will cause in the next few years is terrifying. That's not a question at all. Much has already been said about the question of guilt, but the fact is that the Hypo Alpe-Adria was certainly pursuing a responsible expansion with the help of liabilities of the country of Carinthia under the then head of state Jörg Haider and this circumstance is the origin of the entire miser. The cost of a solution is acceptable only if other scenarios would lead to much higher costs. For this reason, I work with experts on a fast and well-prepared solution for the processing of the non-soldable parts of the Hypo Alpe Adria. The primary objective remains to keep costs for Austrian taxpayers as low as possible. On question 25: Of course, the impact on the government debt ratio and the public deficit after Maastricht can only be explained in detail if the solution model for the Hypo Alpe Adria is determined and concrete. On the question 26: In the government programme, the Federal Government has decided to redesign the basis of the stability levy and to increase the special contribution to the stability levy from 25 percent to 45 percent. Both measures were adopted in the 2014 Tax Amendment Act, which was adopted by a majority yesterday. In this way, Austrian banks would be adequately involved in the cost of saving the Austrian financial sector. On questions 27 to 29: The Austrian Federal Government will present its budget to the High House at the end of April and will choose a forward-looking approach in order to be able to act quickly with possible restructuring needs. Local credit: The municipal loan consists of municipal credit Austria AG, which specializes in the financing of local authorities and no longer lends new loans. No support measures are planned for these in the future. KA Finanz AG is already entrusted with the removal of the remaining risky assets of the municipal credit. This reduction is progressing rapidly and successfully. Over 60 percent of the original assets have already been depleted, and KA Finanz AG is thus a purely decommissioning bank. In the summer of 2013, KA Finanz AG received a shareholder grant of €200 million in order to comply with the capital requirements under the new Basel III regime and further portfolio reduction. In December 2013, a shareholder grant of €150 million was granted for the reduction of the remaining non-Austrian CDS portfolio. To the Volksbanken: I would like to predict that the Austrian banks are not comparable to the Hypo Alpe-Adria. The Volksbanken are strongly rooted in Austria and are largely refinancing through Austrian savings deposits. The top institute ÖVAG has recruited and sold almost all foreign activities. Only the Volksbank Romania and Volksbank Leasing International belong to ÖVAG. On your question about the fresh money. In 2013, ÖVAG received no capital injection and no liquidity aid. Only a guarantee for credit claims of € 100 million was taken over by the Republic. I would like to stress that the ÖVAG is in excess of the essential requirements of the European Commission in the restructuring plan, especially as regards the reduction of assets. Question 30: An examination of these operations is currently under way by the competent authorities or before the ordinary courts. On question 31: One of the main objectives of the Austrian Federal Government is and remains the creation of solid state finances with a balanced budget, a structural zero deficit from 2016 onwards and a reduction in debt levels. In order to achieve this objective, the Federal Government has also presented a tax-amendment law, which was adopted by the majority in the House yesterday. If we have achieved the goal, that is to say, all these measures will work gradually and we will also have a more positive economic environment, then I, as Minister of Finance and Vice-Presidents, will also address the next objective. This is then the relief of taxpayers and also a simplification of the tax system. – Thank you very much.

The second point I would like to draw attention to is that, because much is about detachment of the economy, something is necessary, which goes towards a big throw. If we are already discussing taxes, then we are discussing However, in the area of wage and income tax, the question is: What derogations do we want to eliminate in order to bring about a real reduction in tariffs? A real reduction in tariffs, which would help many small and medium-sized enterprises, but also many people dependent on wages, much more than the abolition of company tax. My considerations are currently heading towards working on something like an integrated tariff, which is supposed to remove most of the existing benefits in wage and income tax law, which then leads to a actually deserved reduction in the income tax and wage tax rate. Companies thus make a tax prepayment on future profits. With the new GmbH-regulatory, the curious situation now arises that this tax is increasing. In other words, companies may not make a profit for years – perhaps because the business plans of start-ups do not at all provide that they make a profit in the first few years – but they always pay higher taxes. The scale of this minimum corporation tax is certainly not threatening to exist, but by paying more and more taxes, one also sets in motion something like a downward spiral. And it is incomprehensible why we unnecessarily make it difficult for companies there. Let us, therefore, make the right decision and finally abolish the minimum corporate tax! This would only be consistent in conjunction with the abolition of company tax. I would like to point out that the payment of the minimum tax should not be the case because it is only to be paid if the corporation generates losses or extremely low profits. However, according to the 2014 tax-amendment law, reliefs should exist for companies with limited liability, which after the 30th anniversary of the tax-amending law. ==References====External links== For these companies a tax-foundation privilege is provided for in the first ten years following entry into the unlimited tax obligation. The minimum tax will be €500 in the first five calendar years, € 1 000 in the following five years. Should profits be achieved during the 10 years of the founding phase, the income tax burden would nevertheless be reduced due to the time and amount limitability of losses and accountability of the minimum taxes already paid on KöSt. In other words, the minimum purchase is, provided that it exceeds the actual annual purchase tax debt, treats as a tax prepayment and is counted on a co-indebted payment in the following years. So here we are talking about a pure advance payment, and this advance payment is not a lost money for the entrepreneur. That is why we will not be approaching your request by the ÖVP, but are still eager to discuss it in the Financial Committee. – Thank you very much.
